Specification family

Hexplain Requirements Vocabulary

Identifies requirements stated by an external standards document, so that executable conformance constraints can cite them. Format-agnostic: it describes what documents demand, independent of any binary format description.

Unofficial working specification. The namespace and version metadata below identify the vocabulary; documentation does not certify an implementation.

Conformance

The key words "MUST", "MUST NOT", "REQUIRED", "SHALL", "SHALL NOT", "SHOULD", "SHOULD NOT", "RECOMMENDED", "MAY", and "OPTIONAL" in this section are to be interpreted as described in BCP 14 (RFC 2119 and RFC 8174) when, and only when, they appear in all capitals, as shown here.

  • A requirement is identified by its IRI. It MUST carry exactly one non-empty req:requirementId, req:fromStandard and req:statement, and exactly one req:discrepancyType.
  • Two standards MAY use the same requirement identifier. Within one graph, two distinct requirements MUST NOT carry the same (req:fromStandard, req:requirementId) pair after surrounding whitespace is removed from both values and req:fromStandard is compared ignoring case; req:RequirementIdentityShape enforces it.
  • req:appliesToVersion values are compared by exact string equality with the input's format version, which the caller of a conformance run supplies; an evaluator MUST NOT infer it (Processing Model §Conformance Evaluation).
  • req:discrepancyType classifies a defect and MUST NOT be used to set a finding's severity; severity is the citing constraint's conf:severity.

Complete term and shape reference

231 named resources are documented below. Definitions and usage notes come from the canonical RDF; range and validation facts are rendered from its axioms and shapes. RDFS/OWL inference, SHACL validation, and editorial usage guidance are shown separately. Blank-node property shapes and logical alternatives are expanded under their owning named shapes.

Constraint language follows W3C SHACL; publication and persistent term identifiers follow the W3C vocabulary publication guidance. Concept definitions and scope notes use SKOS documentation properties.

Shared usage scopes

Shared scope 1
Use as a req:discrepancyType value. It classifies the defect and never sets the severity of a finding.

Hexplain Requirements Vocabulary
Definition
Identifies requirements stated by an external standards document, so that executable conformance constraints can cite them. Format-agnostic: it describes what documents demand, independent of any binary format description.
Usage scope
Import to identify the requirements of a standards document that constraints cite and run reports account for. It describes what documents demand, not how a check is executed.

Class

req:Requirement — Requirement

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/req#Requirement

Label
Requirement
Definition
A single normative requirement drawn from a standards document. A requirement is identified by its IRI. Its (req:fromStandard, req:requirementId) pair describes where it comes from; two different standards may use the same requirement identifier, so the identifier alone identifies nothing, and within one graph the pair (compared after removing surrounding whitespace, with req:fromStandard compared ignoring case) names at most one requirement.
Usage scope
Assert on each requirement a conformance profile cites or declares. Mint one IRI per requirement of the source edition; a later edition's requirement is a different requirement even when its identifier is unchanged.
Declared RDF type
owl:Class
Domain / range
Not applicable to this class. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/req>
Shape usage / validation context
conf:ConstraintShape, conf:DisjointKindsShape, conf:FindingShape, conf:OutcomeShape, conf:ParseAttributionTargetShape, conf:UntypedParseAttributionShape, req:RequirementShape
Cardinality
Context-dependent. The linked shapes state minimum/maximum counts at their value paths; no universal cardinality is inferred.

Node shape

req:RequirementIdentityShape — Requirement identity shape

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/req#RequirementIdentityShape

Label
Requirement identity shape
Definition
Within one graph, a (req:fromStandard, req:requirementId) pair names at most one requirement. The pair is compared after normalising both values (a no-break or other Unicode space counts as a space, zero-width characters and the byte-order mark are removed, and leading and trailing white space is trimmed) and ignoring case in req:fromStandard, so 'MIL-STD-2500C ' and 'mil-std-2500c' are the same standard; the identifier's case is significant. Two distinct requirement resources with the same normalised pair are a violation, reported on each of them. The same identifier under different standards is not.
Declared RDF type
sh:NodeShape
Domain / range
Not applicable to this node shape. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/req>
Activation
the nodes its SPARQL target selects
Severity
SHACL default: sh:Violation. This applies to results of this shape; nested property shapes have their own severity (default sh:Violation), rather than inheriting it. Rules generate triples instead of validation results.
Canonical source
shapes.ttl
Constraint and rule specification
  • rdf:type: sh:NodeShape
  • SPARQL validation constraint:
    • Validation message: "Another req:Requirement ({?other}) has the same req:fromStandard and req:requirementId (after trimming, and ignoring the standard's case); the pair must be unique."
    • SPARQL prefix declarations: req:_prefixes
    • SELECT query returning violations: 
      SELECT $this ?other WHERE {
            $this req:fromStandard ?std ; req:requirementId ?id .
            ?other req:fromStandard ?std2 ; req:requirementId ?id2 .
            FILTER(!sameTerm(?other, $this))
            FILTER(LCASE(REPLACE(REPLACE(REPLACE(STR(?std), "[   -   　]", " "), "[​-‍⁠﻿]", ""), "^[ \t\r\n]+|[ \t\r\n]+$", "")) = LCASE(REPLACE(REPLACE(REPLACE(STR(?std2), "[   -   　]", " "), "[​-‍⁠﻿]", ""), "^[ \t\r\n]+|[ \t\r\n]+$", "")))
            FILTER(REPLACE(REPLACE(REPLACE(STR(?id), "[   -   　]", " "), "[​-‍⁠﻿]", ""), "^[ \t\r\n]+|[ \t\r\n]+$", "") = REPLACE(REPLACE(REPLACE(STR(?id2), "[   -   　]", " "), "[​-‍⁠﻿]", ""), "^[ \t\r\n]+|[ \t\r\n]+$", ""))
        }
  • sh:target:
    • rdf:type: sh:SPARQLTarget
    • SPARQL prefix declarations: req:_prefixes
    • SELECT query returning violations: 
      SELECT DISTINCT ?this WHERE {
            { ?this req:requirementId ?id . ?twin req:requirementId ?id . FILTER(!sameTerm(?twin, ?this)) }
            UNION
            { ?this req:requirementId ?id .
              FILTER(REGEX(STR(?id), "^[ \t\r\n]|[ \t\r\n]$|[   -‍  ⁠　﻿]") || DATATYPE(?id) != <http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#string>) }
            UNION
            { { SELECT ?odd WHERE { ?odd req:requirementId ?oddId .
                  FILTER(REGEX(STR(?oddId), "^[ \t\r\n]|[ \t\r\n]$|[   -‍  ⁠　﻿]") || DATATYPE(?oddId) != <http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#string>) } LIMIT 1 }
              ?this req:requirementId ?id . }
        }
Node shape

req:RequirementShape — Requirement shape

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/req#RequirementShape

Label
Requirement shape
Definition
Applies to instances of req:Requirement; subjects using req:appliesToVersion, req:discrepancyType, req:fromStandard, req:requirementId, req:statement. req:appliesToVersion values are non-empty strings; omit the property to mean every version. A Requirement needs exactly one req:discrepancyType from the controlled set. A Requirement needs exactly one non-blank req:fromStandard. White space, no-break and zero-width spaces alone are blank. A Requirement needs exactly one non-blank req:requirementId. White space, no-break and zero-width spaces alone are blank. A Requirement needs exactly one non-blank req:statement quoting the source text: a plain or language-tagged string.
Declared RDF type
sh:NodeShape
Domain / range
Not applicable to this node shape. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/req>
Activation
instances of req:Requirement; subjects using req:appliesToVersion, req:discrepancyType, req:fromStandard, req:requirementId, req:statement
Severity
SHACL default: sh:Violation. This applies to results of this shape; nested property shapes have their own severity (default sh:Violation), rather than inheriting it. Rules generate triples instead of validation results.
Canonical source
shapes.ttl
Constraint and rule specification
  • rdf:type: sh:NodeShape
  • Property constraint:
    • Required literal datatype (exact SHACL datatype): xsd:string
    • Maximum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Validation message: "A Requirement needs exactly one non-blank req:fromStandard. White space, no-break and zero-width spaces alone are blank."
    • Minimum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Minimum string length: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Value path: req:fromStandard
    • Regular expression: "[^\\t\\n\\r    -‍  ⁠　﻿]"
  • Property constraint:
    • Required literal datatype (exact SHACL datatype): xsd:string
    • Maximum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Validation message: "A Requirement needs exactly one non-blank req:requirementId. White space, no-break and zero-width spaces alone are blank."
    • Minimum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Minimum string length: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Value path: req:requirementId
    • Regular expression: "[^\\t\\n\\r    -‍  ⁠　﻿]"
  • Property constraint:
    • Required literal datatype (exact SHACL datatype): xsd:string
    • Validation message: "req:appliesToVersion values are non-empty strings; omit the property to mean every version."
    • Minimum string length: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Value path: req:appliesToVersion
  • Property constraint:
    • Allowed values:
      1. req:Syntactic
      2. req:Semantic
      3. req:Functional
    • Maximum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Validation message: "A Requirement needs exactly one req:discrepancyType from the controlled set."
    • Minimum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Value path: req:discrepancyType
  • Property constraint:
    • Maximum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Validation message: "A Requirement needs exactly one non-blank req:statement quoting the source text: a plain or language-tagged string."
    • Minimum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • Minimum string length: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>
    • At least one alternative must conform (OR):
        • Required literal datatype (exact SHACL datatype): xsd:string
        • Required literal datatype (exact SHACL datatype): rdf:langString
    • Value path: req:statement
    • Regular expression: "[^\\t\\n\\r    -‍  ⁠　﻿]"
  • Target class: req:Requirement
  • Target subjects of: req:appliesToVersion
  • Target subjects of: req:discrepancyType

Label
Semantic
Definition

Usage scope

Datatype property

req:appliesToVersion — applies to version

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/req#appliesToVersion

Label
applies to version
Definition
A format version the requirement governs (e.g. 'NITF 2.1'). Absence means the requirement applies to every version of the format. Multiple values are a union: the requirement applies to an input whose format version equals any one of them. The input's version is supplied to the conformance run by its caller and compared with each value by exact string equality; when the caller supplies none, every requirement is evaluated and the run reports that version scoping was not applied.
Usage scope
Use zero or more times on a req:Requirement; each value is a non-empty string spelt exactly as callers will supply the version.
Declared RDF type
owl:DatatypeProperty
RDFS domain
No global domain declared. SHACL usage scopes below do not create a global domain axiom.
RDFS range
xsd:string
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/req>

Datatype property

req:requirementId — requirement id

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/req#requirementId

Label
requirement id
Definition
The identifier the source standard uses, verbatim (e.g. 'JBP-2021.2-002'). It is unique only within its standard: two standards may use the same identifier, and the requirement itself is identified by its IRI. Within one graph no two distinct requirements share the same (fromStandard, requirementId) pair after normalisation (see req:Requirement).
Usage scope
Use exactly once on a req:Requirement; a string with at least one visible character (white space, no-break and zero-width spaces alone are blank).
Declared RDF type

Ontology

<https://hexplain.io/ns/req/requirements> — Hexplain Specification Requirements

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/req/requirements

Label
Hexplain Specification Requirements
Definition
The normative requirements that the specification's own SHACL node shapes enforce on a description, each a req:Requirement citing its shape with dcterms:references: one for every named node shape of every module of the family. The source document of a requirement is the vocabulary module that publishes the shape, named without its version; its identifier is req-shape-<prefix>-<ShapeLocalName>. The pair therefore survives a new version of the module and never changes meaning (tools/_requirement_ids.py states the same rule for prose requirements). A conformance profile may cite these requirements with conf:satisfies exactly as it cites a format standard's.
Usage scope
Load with a conformance profile that attributes a description-level finding to the shape requirement it breaks, or to read which requirement a shape stands for. It mints no shapes and no new constraints: every statement restates a shape's documented definition, and the file is regenerated when a shape changes.
Declared RDF type
owl:Ontology
Domain / range
Not applicable to this ontology. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Related resources (no equivalence implied)
<https://hexplain.io/specification/req/requirements-doc.ttl>

Class

reqs:ShapeRequirement — Shape Requirement

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/req/requirements#ShapeRequirement

Label
Shape Requirement
Definition
A req:Requirement that one of the specification's own SHACL node shapes enforces on a description, named by dcterms:references. Its req:fromStandard is the label of the vocabulary module publishing the shape, without a version, and its req:requirementId is req-shape-<prefix>-<ShapeLocalName>. Its req:discrepancyType is derived from the shape: req:Syntactic when every constraint checks one property's values on their own (datatype, node kind, class, enumeration, pattern, bounds, cardinality), req:Semantic when a constraint relates properties or nodes to one another (a logical combination of property constraints, a property pair, a qualified count or a SPARQL constraint), so that each value may be well formed while the description as a whole is not.
Usage scope
Assert on the family's own requirements only; a conformance profile's requirements for a format standard are plain req:Requirement resources. A conf:Constraint may cite a shape requirement with conf:satisfies like any other.
Declared RDF type
owl:Class
Domain / range
Not applicable to this class. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Superclass / class expression
req:Requirement
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/req/requirements>
Shape usage / validation context
No direct named family-shape reference found. This is not a claim that a profile cannot add constraints or reference this term indirectly.
Cardinality
No universal cardinality declared for this term.
Canonical source
requirements.ttl
Declared axioms and values
  • rdf:type: owl:Class
  • rdfs:subClassOf: req:Requirement

A part spec is located exactly one way, by abnd:extension or by abnd:pathPattern: with neither it matches no file, and it never states both. Each focus node is a node that states exactly one of: abnd:extension; abnd:pathPattern. An abnd:extension is a dot followed by one or more non-empty, dot-separated steps (\".dbf\", \".tar.gz\") with no '/', '\\' or ':'; without the dot it would also match a longer suffix such as roads_dbf. abnd:liftsProperty names a property by IRI. abnd:maxParts takes at most one value; each is an integer (of any XSD integer datatype) at least 0. abnd:minParts takes at most one value; each is an integer (of any XSD integer datatype) at least 0. abnd:maxParts must not be less than abnd:minParts. abnd:nestedProfile takes at most one value; each is an instance of abnd:BundleProfile. abnd:partRole names a skos:Concept of the bound part-role register. Each value of abnd:pathPattern is an xsd:string literal. An abnd:pathPattern is a relative path inside the asset root: '/'-separated, non-empty segments, none '.' or '..', with no backslash, no drive letter or other ':', and no leading '/'. A required part spec must not declare abnd:minParts 0 or abnd:maxParts 0 -- the two contradict."@en

The converse is not required -- a date with no change is a review date, which asec:expiresOn already expresses."@en

Each focus node is either a node that states bddo:dataType, a node that states bddo:hasConditionalDataType, a node that states bddo:valueFromExpression or a node that states bddo:hasDispatchTable. bddo:alignment takes at most one value; each is a number greater than 0. bddo:atOffset takes at most one value; each is a number at least 0. bddo:atOffsetFromExpression takes at most one value; each is a HEL expression (an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal). bddo:atOffsetFromField takes at most one value; each is either bddo:MSBFirst or bddo:LSBFirst. bddo:checksum takes at most one value; each is an instance of bddo:Checksum. bddo:dataType takes at most one value; each is an IRI naming an instance of bddo:DataType or bddo:Struct. bddo:encoding takes at most one value; each is a registered bddo:Encoding individual or an instance of bddo:Encoding (a bddo:CustomEncoding a profile mints). bddo:encoding names an encoding outside the registered set (the bddo:Encoding individuals this vocabulary declares); a bddo:CustomEncoding is accepted, but only a processor that supports its bddo:ianaCharset can decode it. bddo:enumeration takes at most one value; each is an instance of bddo:Enumeration. bddo:fixedPointScale takes at most one value. bddo:floatFormat takes at most one value; each is one of bddo:ieee754, bddo:ibmHexadecimal, bddo:vaxF, bddo:vaxD, bddo:vaxG or bddo:bfloat16. bddo:hasDispatchTable takes at most one value; each is an instance of bddo:DispatchTable. bddo:hasFixedValue takes at most one value; each is a literal. bddo:isPresentIf takes at most one value; each is a HEL expression (an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal). bddo:offsetBase takes at most one value; each is one of bddo:streamStart, bddo:streamEnd, bddo:parentStart, bddo:currentPosition or bddo:fieldStart. bddo:offsetBaseField takes at most one value; each is an instance of bddo:Field. bddo:padByte takes at most one value; each is one xsd:hexBinary octet (two hex digits). bddo:padTo takes at most one value; each is a number greater than 0. bddo:repeatCount takes at most one value; each is a number at least 0. bddo:repeatCountFromExpression takes at most one value; each is a HEL expression (an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal). bddo:repeatCountFromField takes at most one value; each is an instance of bddo:Field. bddo:repeatUntil takes at most one value; each is a HEL expression (an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal). bddo:rootKeyFromField takes at most one value; each is an instance of bddo:Field. bddo:seekScope takes at most one value; each is either bddo:regionScope or bddo:streamScope. bddo:separatedBy takes at most one value; each is an xsd:string literal equal to \"whitespace\". bddo:size takes at most one value; each is a number greater than 0. bddo:sizeFromExpression takes at most one value; each is a HEL expression (an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal). bddo:sizeFromField takes at most one value; each is an instance of bddo:Field. bddo:sizeToEndOfStream takes at most one value; each is an xsd:boolean literal. bddo:terminator takes at most one value; each is an xsd:hexBinary literal. bddo:trimNull takes at most one value; each is an xsd:boolean literal. bddo:validIf takes at most one value; each is a HEL expression (an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal). bddo:valueFromExpression takes at most one value; (rdfs:range xsd:positiveInteger). bddo:pathSeparator takes an xsd:string literal (rdfs:range xsd:string). bddo:quoteChar takes an xsd:hexBinary literal (rdfs:range xsd:hexBinary). bddo:recordDelimiter takes an xsd:hexBinary literal (rdfs:range xsd:hexBinary). bddo:repeatCount takes a non-negative integer (rdfs:range xsd:nonNegativeInteger). bddo:repeatCountFromExpression takes a HEL expression, an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal (rdfs:range xsd:string). bddo:repeatUntil takes a HEL expression, an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal (rdfs:range xsd:string). bddo:separatedBy takes an xsd:string literal (rdfs:range xsd:string). bddo:size takes a positive integer (rdfs:range xsd:positiveInteger). bddo:sizeFromExpression takes a HEL expression, an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal (rdfs:range xsd:string). bddo:sizeToEndOfStream takes an xsd:boolean literal (rdfs:range xsd:boolean). bddo:skipRecords takes a non-negative integer (rdfs:range xsd:nonNegativeInteger). bddo:syncOnMarker takes an xsd:hexBinary literal (rdfs:range xsd:hexBinary). bddo:terminator takes an xsd:hexBinary literal (rdfs:range xsd:hexBinary). bddo:trimNull takes an xsd:boolean literal (rdfs:range xsd:boolean). bddo:trimWhitespace takes an xsd:boolean literal (rdfs:range xsd:boolean). bddo:validIf takes a HEL expression, an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal (rdfs:range xsd:string). bddo:valueFromExpression takes a HEL expression, an xsd:string or bddo:HelExpression literal (rdfs:range xsd:string). bddo:whitespaceSeparated takes an xsd:boolean literal (rdfs:range   • rdf:type: owl:NamedIndividual

  • req:discrepancyType: req:Semantic
  • req:fromStandard: "Hexplain Requirements Shapes"
  • req:requirementId: "req-shape-req-RequirementIdentityShape"
  • req:statement: "Within one graph, a (req:fromStandard, req:requirementId) pair names at most one requirement. The pair is compared after normalising both values (a no-break or other Unicode space counts as a space, zero-width characters and the byte-order mark are removed, and leading and trailing white space is trimmed) and ignoring case in req:fromStandard, so 'MIL-STD-2500C ' and 'mil-std-2500c' are the same standard; the identifier's case is significant. Two distinct requirement resources with the same normalised pair are a violation, reported on each of them. The same identifier under different standards is not."@en

Ontology

<https://hexplain.io/ns/req/shapes> — Hexplain Requirements Shapes

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/req/shapes

Label
Hexplain Requirements Shapes
Definition
The SHACL shapes of the req vocabulary, published as their own document so that a consumer can load the vocabulary without its validation rules. Terms stay in the req namespace.
Usage scope
Load with the req vocabulary to validate requirements: a non-blank identifier, standard and statement, one discrepancy type, and one requirement per normalised (standard, identifier) pair.
Declared RDF type
owl:Ontology
Domain / range
Not applicable to this ontology. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Related resources (no equivalence implied)

    rdfs:seeAlso <https://hexplain.io/specification/req/req-doc.ttl> ;

    rdfs:label "Hexplain Requirements Vocabulary"@en ;

    dcterms:modified "2026-10-13"^^xsd:date ;
    dcterms:created "2026-08-01"^^xsd:date ; dcterms:creator <https://geoknoesis.com> ;
    dcterms:license <https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/> ;
    vann:preferredNamespacePrefix "req" ; vann:preferredNamespaceUri "https://hexplain.io/ns/req#" ;
    rdfs:comment """Identifies requirements stated by an external standards document, so that
executable conformance constraints can cite them. Format-agnostic: it describes what documents
demand, independent of any binary format description."""@en .

# --- Classes ---
:Requirement a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Requirement"@en ;
    rdfs:comment "A single normative requirement drawn from a standards document. A requirement is identified by its IRI. Its (req:fromStandard, req:requirementId) pair describes where it comes from; two different standards may use the same requirement identifier, so the identifier alone identifies nothing, and within one graph the pair (compared after removing surrounding whitespace, with req:fromStandard compared ignoring case) names at most one requirement."@en ;
    skos:scopeNote "Assert on each requirement a conformance profile cites or declares. Mint one IRI per requirement of the source edition; a later edition's requirement is a different requirement even when its identifier is unchanged."@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/req> .

:DiscrepancyType a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Discrepancy Type"@en ;
    rdfs:comment "The class of defect that violating a requirement produces."@en ;
    skos:scopeNote "The class of the three controlled values of req:discrepancyType; do not mint further types."@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/req> .

# --- Controlled individuals ---
# Definitions track the discrepancy types used by conformance test reporting: a violation
# impairs interpretability (syntactic), understandability (semantic), or usability (functional).
:Syntactic a owl:NamedIndividual , :DiscrepancyType ; skos:scopeNote "Use as a req:discrepancyType value. It classifies the defect and never sets the severity of a finding."@en ; rdfs:label "Syntactic"@en ;
    rdfs:comment "Impacts interpretability of the data: violates format or compression specifications."@en .
:Semantic a owl:NamedIndividual , :DiscrepancyType ; skos:scopeNote "Use as a req:discrepancyType value. It classifies the defect and never sets the severity of a finding."@en ; rdfs:label "Semantic"@en ;
    rdfs:comment "Impacts understandability of the data: violates data dictionaries or content specifications."@en .
:Functional a owl:NamedIndividual , :DiscrepancyType ; skos:scopeNote "Use as a req:discrepancyType value. It classifies the defect and never sets the severity of a finding."@en ; rdfs:label "Functional"@en ;
    rdfs:comment "Impacts usability of the data: violates community or system documentation and data models."@en .

# --- Properties ---
:requirementId a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "requirement id"@en ;
    rdfs:comment "The identifier the source standard uses, verbatim (e.g. 'JBP-2021.2-002'). It is unique only within its standard: two standards may use the same identifier, and the requirement itself is identified by its IRI. Within one graph no two distinct requirements share the same (fromStandard, requirementId) pair after normalisation (see req:Requirement)."@en ;
    skos:scopeNote "Use exactly once on a req:Requirement; a string with at least one visible character (white space, no-break and zero-width spaces alone are blank)."@en ;
    rdfs:range xsd:string .

:fromStandard a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "from standard"@en ;
    rdfs:comment "Source document and edition (e.g. 'MIL-STD-2500C, 01 May 2006')."@en ;
    skos:scopeNote "Use exactly once on a req:Requirement; a non-empty string naming the document and its edition. It is compared ignoring case and surrounding whitespace when requirement identity is checked."@en ;
    rdfs:range xsd:string .

:statement a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "statement"@en ;
    rdfs:comment "The requirement text, quoted verbatim from the source: a plain string, or a language-tagged string stating the source's language."@en ;
    skos:scopeNote "Use exactly once on a req:Requirement; a non-blank xsd:string or rdf:langString."@en ;
    rdfs:range rdfs:Literal .

:discrepancyType a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "discrepancy type"@en ;
    rdfs:comment "The class of defect a violation produces."@en ;
    skos:scopeNote "Use exactly once on a req:Requirement, with one of req:Syntactic, req:Semantic or req:Functional."@en ;
    rdfs:range :DiscrepancyType .

:appliesToVersion a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "applies to version"@en ;
    rdfs:comment "A format version the requirement governs (e.g. 'NITF 2.1'). Absence means the requirement applies to every version of the format. Multiple values are a union: the requirement applies to an input whose format version equals any one of them. The input's version is supplied to the conformance run by its caller and compared with each value by exact string equality; when the caller supplies none, every requirement is evaluated and the run reports that version scoping was not applied."@en ;
    skos:scopeNote "Use zero or more times on a req:Requirement; each value is a non-empty string spelt exactly as callers will supply the version."@en ;
    rdfs:range xsd:string .

# Generated by tools/_build_shape_requirements.py --write; do not edit. One reqs:ShapeRequirement
# per named node shape of every family module, derived from the shape: its statement is the
# shape's documented definition, its identifier req-shape-<prefix>-<ShapeLocalName>, its source
# the module's label without a version. tools/test_shape_requirements.py keeps the two in step.

    rdfs:comment "The normative requirements that the specification's own SHACL node shapes enforce on a description, each a req:Requirement citing its shape with dcterms:references: one for every named node shape of every module of the family. The source document of a requirement is the vocabulary module that publishes the shape, named without its version; its identifier is req-shape-<prefix>-<ShapeLocalName>. The pair therefore survives a new version of the module and never changes meaning (tools/_requirement_ids.py states the same rule for prose requirements). A conformance profile may cite these requirements with conf:satisfies exactly as it cites a format standard's."@en ;
    skos:scopeNote "Load with a conformance profile that attributes a description-level finding to the shape requirement it breaks, or to read which requirement a shape stands for. It mints no shapes and no new constraints: every statement restates a shape's documented definition, and the file is regenerated when a shape changes."@en .

# A requirement of the specification itself, as opposed to one a conformance profile declares
# for a format standard. A subclass, and the individuals are typed with it alone: conf:RunShape
# demands an outcome for every resource typed req:Requirement in the graph it validates (a run
# report with its rule set), and the family's own requirements are not part of any rule set.
# Under RDFS entailment each is a req:Requirement; req:RequirementShape still validates each
# through its activation by req:requirementId and the other requirement properties.
reqs:ShapeRequirement a owl:Class ; rdfs:subClassOf req:Requirement ; rdfs:label "Shape Requirement"@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/req/requirements> ;
    rdfs:comment "A req:Requirement that one of the specification's own SHACL node shapes enforces on a description, named by dcterms:references. Its req:fromStandard is the label of the vocabulary module publishing the shape, without a version, and its req:requirementId is req-shape-<prefix>-<ShapeLocalName>. Its req:discrepancyType is derived from the shape: req:Syntactic when every constraint checks one property's values on their own (datatype, node kind, class, enumeration, pattern, bounds, cardinality), req:Semantic when a constraint relates properties or nodes to one another (a logical combination of property constraints, a property pair, a qualified count or a SPARQL constraint), so that each value may be well formed while the description as a whole is not."@en ;
    skos:scopeNote "Assert on the family's own requirements only; a conformance profile's requirements for a format standard are plain req:Requirement resources. A conf:Constraint may cite a shape requirement with conf:satisfies like any other."@en .

reqs:req-shape-abnd-PartSpecShape a owl:NamedIndividual , reqs:ShapeRequirement ;
    rdfs:label "Requirement of abnd:PartSpecShape"@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/req/requirements> ;
    rdfs:comment "The requirement abnd:PartSpecShape enforces on a description; a description that this shape reports does not satisfy it."@en ;
    req:requirementId "req-shape-abnd-PartSpecShape" ;
    req:fromStandard "Hexplain Aspect — Bundle (hx-bundle)" ;
    req:statement "Applies to instances of abnd:PartSpec; objects of abnd:partSpec. A part spec is located exactly one way, by abnd:extension or by abnd:pathPattern: with neither it matches no file, and it never states both. Each focus node is a node that states exactly one of: abnd:extension; abnd:pathPattern. An abnd:extension is a dot followed by one or more non-empty, dot-separated steps (\".dbf\", \".tar.gz\") with no '/', '\\' or ':'; without the dot it would also match a longer suffix such as roads_dbf. abnd:liftsProperty names a property by IRI. abnd:maxParts takes at most one value; each is an integer (of any XSD integer datatype) at least 0. abnd:minParts takes at most one value; each is an integer (of any XSD integer datatype) at least 0. abnd:maxParts must not be less than abnd:minParts. abnd:nestedProfile takes at most one value; each is an instance of abnd:BundleProfile. abnd:partRole names a skos:Concept of the bound part-role register. Each value of abnd:pathPattern is an xsd:string literal. An abnd:pathPattern is a relative path inside the asset root: '/'-separated, non-empty segments, none '.' or '..', with no backslash, no drive letter or other ':', and no leading '/'. A required part spec must not declare abnd:minParts 0 or abnd:maxParts 0 -- the two contradict."@en ;
    req:discrepancyType req:Semantic ;
    dcterms:references abnd:PartSpecShape .

<https://hexplain.io/ns/req/shapes> a owl:Ontology ;
    rdfs:seeAlso <https://hexplain.io/specification/req/shapes-doc.ttl> ;
    owl:versionIRI <https://hexplain.io/ns/req/shapes/1.3> ;
    owl:priorVersion <https://hexplain.io/ns/req/shapes/1.2> ;
    owl:versionInfo "1.3" ;
    dcterms:modified "2026-10-13"^^xsd:date ;
    rdfs:label "Hexplain Requirements Shapes"@en ;
    rdfs:comment "The SHACL shapes of the req vocabulary, published as their own document so that a consumer can load the vocabulary without its validation rules. Terms stay in the req namespace."@en ;
    owl:imports <https://hexplain.io/ns/req> ;
    # No vann:preferredNamespacePrefix: the shapes' terms are in the vocabulary's namespace, and
    # only the vocabulary claims its prefix.

:RequirementShape a sh:NodeShape ;
    sh:targetClass req:Requirement ;
    # Activated by its own properties too, so omitting rdf:type does not bypass it.
    sh:targetSubjectsOf req:requirementId , req:fromStandard , req:statement , req:discrepancyType , req:appliesToVersion ;
    rdfs:label "Requirement shape"@en ;
    sh:property [
        sh:path req:requirementId ;
        sh:datatype xsd:string ;
        sh:minCount 1 ; sh:maxCount 1 ;
        sh:minLength 1 ;
        # At least one character that is not white space or invisible (no-break and zero-width
        # spaces, byte-order mark): an identifier of blanks names nothing.
        sh:pattern "[^\\t\\n\\r \u00A0\u1680\u2000-\u200D\u202F\u205F\u2060\u3000\uFEFF]" ;
        sh:message "A Requirement needs exactly one non-blank req:requirementId. White space, no-break and zero-width spaces alone are blank." ;
    ] ;
    sh:property [
        sh:path req:fromStandard ;
        sh:datatype xsd:string ;
        sh:minCount 1 ; sh:maxCount 1 ;
        sh:minLength 1 ;
        # At least one character that is not white space or invisible (no-break and zero-width
        # spaces, byte-order mark): an identifier of blanks names nothing.
        sh:pattern "[^\\t\\n\\r \u00A0\u1680\u2000-\u200D\u202F\u205F\u2060\u3000\uFEFF]" ;
        sh:message "A Requirement needs exactly one non-blank req:fromStandard. White space, no-break and zero-width spaces alone are blank." ;
    ] ;
    sh:property [
        sh:path req:statement ;
        sh:or ( [ sh:datatype xsd:string ] [ sh:datatype rdf:langString ] ) ;
        sh:minCount 1 ; sh:maxCount 1 ;
        sh:minLength 1 ;
        sh:pattern "[^\\t\\n\\r \u00A0\u1680\u2000-\u200D\u202F\u205F\u2060\u3000\uFEFF]" ;
        sh:message "A Requirement needs exactly one non-blank req:statement quoting the source text: a plain or language-tagged string." ;
    ] ;
    sh:property [
        sh:path req:discrepancyType ;
        sh:minCount 1 ; sh:maxCount 1 ;
        sh:in ( req:Syntactic req:Semantic req:Functional ) ;
        sh:message "A Requirement needs exactly one req:discrepancyType from the controlled set." ;
    ] ;
    sh:property [
        sh:path req:appliesToVersion ;
        sh:datatype xsd:string ;
        sh:minLength 1 ;
        sh:message "req:appliesToVersion values are non-empty strings; omit the property to mean every version." ;
    ] .

# Prefixes for the SPARQL-based constraints below (same pattern as bddo:_prefixes).
req:_prefixes sh:declare
    [ sh:prefix "req" ; sh:namespace "https://hexplain.io/ns/req#"^^xsd:anyURI ] .

:RequirementIdentityShape a sh:NodeShape ;
    # The focus nodes are the requirements that CAN share their normalised pair with another,
    # found once per validation rather than by comparing every pair: the constraint below ran
    # twelve regular-expression replacements for each pair of requirements in the graph, which
    # is quadratic, and a graph holding the family's own shape requirements has hundreds. The
    # normalisation changes only leading or trailing white space, Unicode spaces and zero-width
    # characters, so two plain-string identifiers holding none of them are equal after it
    # exactly when they are the same literal. A requirement can therefore have a twin only if
    # another requirement states the very same identifier (first branch, an index join), or its
    # own identifier holds such a character or is not an xsd:string (second branch), or some
    # other requirement's does (third branch: then every requirement is a candidate). Every
    # requirement the constraint could report is selected, so what is reported is unchanged.
    sh:target [ a sh:SPARQLTarget ;
        sh:prefixes req:_prefixes ;
        sh:select """SELECT DISTINCT ?this WHERE {
            { ?this req:requirementId ?id . ?twin req:requirementId ?id . FILTER(!sameTerm(?twin, ?this)) }
            UNION
            { ?this req:requirementId ?id .
              FILTER(REGEX(STR(?id), "^[ \\t\\r\\n]|[ \\t\\r\\n]$|[\u00A0\u1680\u2000-\u200D\u202F\u205F\u2060\u3000\uFEFF]") || DATATYPE(?id) != <http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#string>) }
            UNION
            { { SELECT ?odd WHERE { ?odd req:requirementId ?oddId .
                  FILTER(REGEX(STR(?oddId), "^[ \\t\\r\\n]|[ \\t\\r\\n]$|[\u00A0\u1680\u2000-\u200D\u202F\u205F\u2060\u3000\uFEFF]") || DATATYPE(?oddId) != <http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#string>) } LIMIT 1 }
              ?this req:requirementId ?id . }
        }""" ] ;
    rdfs:label "Requirement identity shape"@en ;
    rdfs:comment "Within one graph, a (req:fromStandard, req:requirementId) pair names at most one requirement. The pair is compared after normalising both values (a no-break or other Unicode space counts as a space, zero-width characters and the byte-order mark are removed, and leading and trailing white space is trimmed) and ignoring case in req:fromStandard, so 'MIL-STD-2500C ' and 'mil-std-2500c' are the same standard; the identifier's case is significant. Two distinct requirement resources with the same normalised pair are a violation, reported on each of them. The same identifier under different standards is not."@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/req> ;
    sh:sparql [
        sh:message "Another req:Requirement ({?other}) has the same req:fromStandard and req:requirementId (after trimming, and ignoring the standard's case); the pair must be unique." ;
        sh:prefixes req:_prefixes ;
        sh:select """SELECT $this ?other WHERE {
            $this req:fromStandard ?std ; req:requirementId ?id .
            ?other req:fromStandard ?std2 ; req:requirementId ?id2 .
            FILTER(!sameTerm(?other, $this))
            FILTER(LCASE(REPLACE(REPLACE(REPLACE(STR(?std), "[\u00A0\u1680\u2000-\u200A\u202F\u205F\u3000]", " "), "[\u200B-\u200D\u2060\uFEFF]", ""), "^[ \\t\\r\\n]+|[ \\t\\r\\n]+$", "")) = LCASE(REPLACE(REPLACE(REPLACE(STR(?std2), "[\u00A0\u1680\u2000-\u200A\u202F\u205F\u3000]", " "), "[\u200B-\u200D\u2060\uFEFF]", ""), "^[ \\t\\r\\n]+|[ \\t\\r\\n]+$", "")))
            FILTER(REPLACE(REPLACE(REPLACE(STR(?id), "[\u00A0\u1680\u2000-\u200A\u202F\u205F\u3000]", " "), "[\u200B-\u200D\u2060\uFEFF]", ""), "^[ \\t\\r\\n]+|[ \\t\\r\\n]+$", "") = REPLACE(REPLACE(REPLACE(STR(?id2), "[\u00A0\u1680\u2000-\u200A\u202F\u205F\u3000]", " "), "[\u200B-\u200D\u2060\uFEFF]", ""), "^[ \\t\\r\\n]+|[ \\t\\r\\n]+$", ""))
        }""" ;
    ] .

# ---------- Range datatype shapes ----------
# Each XSD rdfs:range above, enforced wherever the property is used: activation is by
# subjects-of, so an untyped subject is checked too (tools/test_range_datatypes.py).
# Integer ranges accept any XSD integer-family datatype within the range's bounds.
req:RangeDatatypeShape a sh:NodeShape ; rdfs:label "Range datatype shape"@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/req> ;
    sh:targetSubjectsOf req:appliesToVersion ,
        req:fromStandard ,
        req:requirementId ;
    sh:property [ sh:path req:appliesToVersion ; sh:datatype xsd:string ;
        sh:message "req:appliesToVersion takes an xsd:string literal (rdfs:range xsd:string)." ] ;
    sh:property [ sh:path req:fromStandard ; sh:datatype xsd:string ;
        sh:message "req:fromStandard takes an xsd:string literal (rdfs:range xsd:string)." ] ;
    sh:property [ sh:path req:requirementId ; sh:datatype xsd:string ;
        sh:message "req:requirementId takes an xsd:string literal (rdfs:range xsd:string)." ] .

# ---------- Term ownership ----------
# rdfs:isDefinedBy for the terms whose declaration above does not state it. A new term states
# its own; tools/test_term_reference.py requires it of every term this file defines.
:RequirementShape rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/req> .
:_prefixes rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/req> .
